For a parallelogram with sides u and v, the equations of the sides can be written as follows.\n\nIf the parallelogram is formed by vectors u = (a, 0) and v = (b, c), then the sides are:\n\nAB: from (0,0) to (a,0); equation y =0.\n\nAD: from (0,0) to (b, c); equation c x - b y =0.\n\nBC: from (a,0) to (a + b, c); equation c(x - a) - b y = c^2.\n\nCD: from (a + b, c) to (b, c); equation y = c.\n\nThus, the distances from point P(x, y) to each side are:\n\n- Distance to AB: h1 = y.\n\n- Distance to AD: h2 = |c x - b y| / sqrt(b^2 + c^2).\n\n- Distance to BC: h3 = |c(x - a) - b y| / sqrt(b^2 + c^2).\n\n- Distance to CD: h4 = c - y.\n\nGiven in the problem, these distances are 1, 2, 3, 4 in some order, with h1 + h4 =5 and h2 + h3=5. Since h4 = c - y, then h1 + h4 = y + c - y =c =5. Therefore, the height between AB and CD is 5. Thus, c=5.\n\nTherefore, h2 and h3 must sum to 5, and h2 and h3 are expressed as |c x - b y| / sqrt(b^2 + c^2) and |c(x - a) - b y| / sqrt(b^2 + c^2) with c=5. Therefore, h2 + h3 =5.\n\nSo, substituting c=5, h2 and h3 are |5x - b y| / sqrt(b^2 +25) and |5(x - a) - b y| / sqrt(b^2 +25). Thus,\n\n(|5x - b y| + |5(x - a) - b y| ) / sqrt(b^2 +25) =5.\n\nWe need to find a, b such that this equation can hold for some x in [0, a + b] and y in [0, c] = [0,5].\n\nSimilarly, the distances h1 and h4 are y and 5 - y, where y is between 0 and5.\n\nGiven that, we need to have h2 and h3 (the non-vertical distances) sum to 5. Let's multiply numerator and denominator by the conjugate to make it easier to handle:\n   \\[\n   x_k = \\frac{-3 + \\sqrt{8k + 1}}{4} = \\frac{(-3 + \\sqrt{8k + 1})(4 + \\sqrt{8k + 1})}{4(4 + \\sqrt{8k + 1})} = \\frac{(-3)(4) + (-3)(\\sqrt{8k + 1}) + (\\sqrt{8k + 1})(4) + (\\sqrt{8k + 1})^2}{16 + 4\\sqrt{8k + 1}}\n   \\]\n   After simplifying, the numerator becomes:\n   \\[\n   -12 - 3\\sqrt{8k + 1} + 4\\sqrt{8k + 1} + (8k + 1) = (-12 + 1) + (-3 + 4)\\sqrt{8k + 1} + 8k\n   \\]\n   \\[\n   = 8k - 11 + \\sqrt{8k + 1}\n   \\]\n   Therefore, the denominator is \\(16 + 4\\sqrt{8k + 1}\\). So:\n   \\[\n   x_k = \\frac{8k - 11 + \\sqrt{8k + 1}}{16 + 4\\sqrt{8k + 1}} = \\frac{(8k - 11) + \\sqrt{8k + 1}}{4(4 + \\sqrt{8k + 1})}\n   \\]\n   Alternatively, perhaps another approach can be taken. Let's consider solving the equation for \\( x \\) such that \\( 4x + 3 = \\sqrt{8k + 1} \\). Then squaring both sides:\n   \\[\n   (4x + 3)^2 = 8k + 1 \\\\\n   16x^2 + 24x + 9 = 8k + 1 \\\\\n   16x^2 + 24x + 8 = 8k \\\\\n   k = 2x^2 + 3x + 1\n   \\]\n   So, if we let \\( x \\) be an integer, then \\( k = 2x^2 + 3x + 1 \\). Therefore, for each integer \\( x \\), \\( k \\) can be expressed in terms of \\( x \\). However, this might help in relating \\( k \\) and \\( x \\).\n\n3. **Relate the expression to integer values**:\n   Let's set \\( n = \\lfloor x_k \\rfloor \\). Then, \\( x_k \\) lies in the interval \\( [n, n + 1) \\). So:\n   \\[\n   n \\leq \\frac{-3 + \\sqrt{8k + 1}}{4} < n + 1\n   \\]\n   Multiplying all parts by 4:\n   \\[\n   4n \\leq -3 + \\sqrt{8k + 1} < 4n + 4\n   \\]\n   Adding 3 to all parts:\n   \\[\n   4n + 3 \\leq \\sqrt{8k + 1} < 4n + 7\n   \\]\n   Squaring all parts (since square root is increasing):\n   \\[\n   (4n + 3)^2 \\leq 8k + 1 < (4n + 7)^2\n   \\]\n   Subtracting 1:\n   \\[\n   (4n + 3)^2 - 1 \\leq 8k < (4n + 7)^2 - 1\n   \\]\n   Simplifying:\n   \\[\n   (4n + 4)^2 - 1 \\leq 8k < (4n + 7)^2 - 1\n   \\]\n   Let's compute these expressions:\n   \\[\n   (4n + 4)^2 - 1 = 16n^2 + 32n + 16 - 1 = 16n^2 + 32n + 15\n   \\]\n   \\[\n   (4n + 7)^2 - 1 = 16n^2 + 56n + 49 - 1 = 16n^2 + 56n + 48\n   \\]\n   Therefore:\n   \\[\n   16n^2 + 32n + 15 \\leq 8k < 16n^2 + 56n + 48\n   \\]\n   Dividing all parts by 8:\n   \\[\n   2n^2 + 4n + \\frac{15}{8} \\leq k < 2n^2 + 7n + \\frac{48}{8} = 2n^2 + 7n + 6\n   \\]\n   Since \\( k \\) is an integer, the inequality implies that \\( k \\) lies between \\( 2n^2 + 4n + 2 \\) and \\( 2n^2 + 7n + 5 \\), but we need to check the exact bounds.\n\n   Let's check the exact inequalities:\n   \\[\n   2n^2 + 4n + \\frac{15}{8} \\leq k < 2n^2 + 7n + 6\n   \\]\n   Converting the fractional part to integers:\n   \\[\n   2n^2 + 4n + 15/8 = 2n^2 + 4n + 1 + 7/8\n   \\]\n   Therefore, the integer part starts at \\( 2n^2 + 4n + 1 \\) and goes up to \\( 2n^2 + 7n + 5 \\).\n\n   Wait, but let's verify with specific values.
